Lethal Congenital Contracture Syndrome

Mendelian MONDO:0017436 Pathograph 59 Show in embeddings browser Congenital Disorder

Lethal congenital contracture syndrome (LCCS) comprises genetically heterogeneous autosomal recessive disorders with prenatal akinesia and congenital contractures. The numbered subtypes involve GLE1-associated spinal motor-neuron development, ERBB3 signaling, PIP5K1C-dependent phosphoinositide metabolism, MYBPC1-associated muscle function, DNM2-dependent trafficking, ZBTB42-associated muscle development, CNTNAP1 paranodal junctions, ADCY6 and ADGRG6 peripheral myelination, NEK9-associated skeletal development, GLDN nodal organization, and combined neuronal and muscle DST isoforms. Some mechanistic steps are inferred from cellular or animal models and should not be transferred between subtypes. Reduced movement can produce joint deformations, impaired fetal swallowing and pulmonary hypoplasia, but primary skeletal abnormalities and central nervous system involvement also occur. Prenatal and neonatal mortality is high in selected series; GLDN- and CNTNAP1-associated disease can permit longer survival with substantial morbidity.

Inheritance

1
Autosomal recessive inheritance HP:0000007
Every established LCCS locus is inherited in an autosomal recessive manner, with homozygous founder alleles in consanguineous or geographically isolated kindreds and compound heterozygosity elsewhere.
Autosomal recessive inheritance
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:25055871 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Lethal congenital contracture syndrome (LCCS) is a lethal autosomal recessive form of arthrogryposis multiplex congenita (AMC)."
States the autosomal recessive mode of inheritance for the LCCS group as a whole.
◆

Subtypes

12
LCCS1 (GLE1-related, Finnish type / Herva disease) MONDO:0009670
GLE1 hgnc:4315 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in GLE1 (hgnc:4315). hgnc:4315 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
The Finnish-heritage form, caused by biallelic GLE1 variants (classically the FinMajor splice allele). It is the most severe subtype: total fetal immobility detectable from the first trimester, hydrops, pterygia, and fetal death typically before 32 weeks, with autopsy showing loss of anterior horn motoneurons and severe ventral spinal cord atrophy.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:18204449 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Here, we report that the defective gene underlying lethal motoneuron syndrome LCCS1 is the mRNA export mediator GLE1."
Establishes GLE1 as the LCCS1 gene.
LCCS2 (ERBB3-related) MONDO:0011868
ERBB3 hgnc:3431 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in ERBB3 (hgnc:3431). hgnc:3431 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
ERBB3-associated disease in an Israeli Bedouin kindred combines neurogenic arthrogryposis, anterior-horn atrophy and marked urinary-bladder distension. The founder splice variant predicts a truncated receptor. Impaired PI3K/Akt signaling and deficient Schwann-cell support are proposed contributors based partly on Erbb3-null mice; the complete human pathway is unresolved.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:17701904 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We show that the disease is caused by aberrant splicing of ERBB3, which leads to a predicted truncated protein."
Establishes ERBB3 as the LCCS2 gene.
LCCS3 (PIP5K1C-related) MONDO:0012656
PIP5K1C hgnc:8996 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in PIP5K1C (hgnc:8996). hgnc:8996 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
PIP5K1C-associated arthrogryposis resembles the original ERBB3 kindred clinically but lacks its neurogenic bladder finding. The original D253N allele markedly reduces recombinant kinase activity. Impaired PIP2-dependent synaptic vesicle trafficking is a proposed route to neuronal dysfunction, but no postmortem examinations were performed in the original LCCS3 series, so shared anterior-horn pathology cannot be assumed. Additional alleles and populations have since been reported.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:17701898 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We now describe a third LCCS phenotype (LCCS3)--similar to LCCS2 yet without neurogenic bladder."
Defines LCCS3 and its clinical discriminator from LCCS2.
LCCS4 (MYBPC1-related) MONDO:0013965
MYBPC1 hgnc:7549 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in MYBPC1 (hgnc:7549). hgnc:7549 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Recessive truncation of MYBPC1 abrogates the functional domains of slow-type myosin-binding protein C in the original LCCS4 families. A muscle contractile mechanism is plausible from the protein function; human myofibrillar histopathology was not established by the quoted genetic report. Other MYBPC1 alleles cause nonlethal distal or generalized arthrogryposis.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:22610851 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We now show that a recessive mutation abrogating all functional domains in the same gene leads to LCCS."
Establishes recessive MYBPC1 loss of function as the LCCS4 mechanism.
LCCS5 (DNM2-related, fetal akinesia with cerebral and retinal hemorrhage) MONDO:0014149
DNM2 hgnc:2974 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in DNM2 (hgnc:2974). hgnc:2974 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Caused by a homozygous DNM2 p.Phe379Val substitution impairing endocytosis. Distinguished from the other subtypes by intracranial and retinal haemorrhages alongside the akinesia and contractures, consistent with a pleiotropic dynamin-2 requirement in both muscle and vascular development.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:23092955 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We report the first homozygous mutation in the DNM2 protein p.Phe379Val, in three consanguineous patients with a lethal congenital syndrome associating akinesia, joint contractures, hypotonia, skeletal abnormalities, and brain and retinal hemorrhages."
Establishes DNM2 and the distinguishing haemorrhagic phenotype.
LCCS6 (ZBTB42-related) MONDO:0014549
ZBTB42 hgnc:32550 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in ZBTB42 (hgnc:32550). hgnc:32550 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Identified by autozygome analysis in a consanguineous Saudi family with recurrent stillbirths. ZBTB42 is enriched in skeletal muscle, particularly at the neuromuscular junction, and zebrafish knockdown reproduces disorganized myofibrils - placing the lesion at the muscle/NMJ end of the motor unit.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:25055871 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Our data assign a novel muscular developmental phenotype to ZBTB42 in vertebrates and establish a new LCCS6 type caused by ZBTB42 mutation."
Establishes ZBTB42 as the LCCS6 gene.
LCCS7 (CNTNAP1-related) MONDO:0014569
CNTNAP1 hgnc:8011 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in CNTNAP1 (hgnc:8011). hgnc:8011 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Caused by biallelic CNTNAP1 loss of function. CASPR is an essential paranodal component, and its loss produces a developmental nodopathy with motor nerve conduction velocities below 10 m/s and grossly abnormal nodes of Ranvier on sciatic nerve electron microscopy.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:24319099 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Homozygous frameshift mutations in CNTNAP1 were found in four unrelated families."
Establishes biallelic CNTNAP1 loss of function as an LCCS cause.
LCCS8 (ADCY6-related) MONDO:0014570
ADCY6 hgnc:237 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in ADCY6 (hgnc:237). hgnc:237 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Caused by biallelic ADCY6 variants. Adenylate cyclase 6 generates the cAMP signal that drives Schwann cell myelination, and affected nerves lack peripheral myelin entirely on electron microscopy.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:24319099 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"A homozygous missense mutation in adenylate cyclase 6 gene (ADCY6) was found in another family characterized by a lack of myelin in the peripheral nervous system (PNS) as determined by TEM."
Establishes ADCY6 and the amyelinating peripheral nerve phenotype.
LCCS9 (ADGRG6/GPR126-related) MONDO:0014670
ADGRG6 hgnc:13841 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in ADGRG6 (hgnc:13841). hgnc:13841 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Caused by biallelic ADGRG6 (GPR126) variants. GPR126 is an adhesion GPCR required for Schwann cell myelination; affected peripheral nerves lack myelin basic protein, and Gpr126-null mice reproduce the lethal arthrogryposis phenotype.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:26004201 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"By whole-exome or targeted exome sequencing, it was shown that the probands each harbored a different homozygous mutation (one missense, one nonsense, and one frameshift mutation) in GPR126."
Establishes biallelic GPR126/ADGRG6 variants as a cause of lethal AMC.
PMID:26004201 SUPPORT Model Organism
"A previous study reported that Gpr126(-/-) mice have a lethal arthrogryposis phenotype."
The mouse knockout evidence the subtype description relies on for recapitulation.
LCCS10 (NEK9-related) MONDO:0014870
NEK9 hgnc:18591 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in NEK9 (hgnc:18591). hgnc:18591 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
NEK9-associated lethal skeletal dysplasia was initially reported in two Irish Traveller families with homozygous p.Arg497*, fetal akinesia, shortened long bones, rib and thoracic abnormalities, contractures and pulmonary hypoplasia. Patient fibroblasts show cell-cycle and ciliary abnormalities. These support a developmental mechanism without establishing how much each skeletal finding reflects primary dysplasia versus impaired movement. Later surviving APUG-like neonatal cases are a distinct allelic presentation.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:26908619 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We report two Irish Traveller families with a previously undescribed lethal skeletal dysplasia characterized by fetal akinesia, shortening of all long bones, multiple contractures, rib anomalies, thoracic dysplasia, pulmonary hypoplasia and protruding abdomen."
The original lethal skeletal-dysplasia families establish the LCCS10 clinical scope; the later APUG-like neonates are not used as its denominator.
LCCS11 (GLDN-related) MONDO:0014965
GLDN hgnc:29514 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in GLDN (hgnc:29514). hgnc:29514 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Caused by biallelic GLDN variants. Gliomedin is the Schwann-cell-derived ligand that clusters sodium channels at nascent nodes of Ranvier through neurofascin-186, so its loss is a second developmental nodopathy alongside LCCS7. LCCS11 is the subtype with the best-documented survival beyond the neonatal period.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:27616481 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Through linkage analysis, homozygosity mapping, and exome sequencing in four unrelated families affected by lethal AMC, we identified biallelic mutations in GLDN in the affected individuals."
Establishes biallelic GLDN variants as the LCCS11 cause.
LCCS12 MONDO:0981031
DST hgnc:1090 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this subtype is caused by variation in this gene This subtype is caused by variation in DST (hgnc:1090). hgnc:1090 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
The lethal congenital-contracture presentation is associated with biallelic DST variants affecting both neuronal DST-a and muscle DST-b isoforms. Four patients from two families had severe arthrogryposis and intrauterine or early postnatal death. The separate 19-patient DST-b-only congenital-myopathy cohort cannot establish the muscle histology, cardiomyopathy frequency or survival of LCCS12.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:40497796 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Genotype-phenotype correlation in these patients and previously published cases with respective variants resulted in the definition of a DST-associated lethal congenital contracture syndrome."
Establishes DST as the gene defining a lethal congenital contracture syndrome.
PMID:40497796 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Variants that also impact DST-a besides DST-b result in a more severe, lethal congenital contracture syndrome."
Gives the isoform-dependent severity relationship that separates the lethal phenotype from the congenital myopathy.

Discussions and Knowledge Gaps

12
Which variant, developmental and care factors determine survival and later progression in GLDN-associated disease?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN lccs_survival_variability
Attached to
has_subtypes#LCCS11 phenotypes#Neonatal Respiratory Failure
Published GLDN series include terminations, neonatal deaths and long survivors, with differing denominators and support. The 2025 founder report includes four early deaths and one survivor with later progressive compromise. These data establish variable outcome but do not separate residual function, modifiers and care effects.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39713852 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Our data corroborate those of other reports, demonstrating that LCCS11 is not universally lethal, but long-term survivors are at risk of progressive neuromuscular compromise."
The newer founder series independently supports variable lethality and later morbidity.
How do combined neuronal and muscle dystonin defects produce the lethal contracture phenotype?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN lccs12_tissue_attribution
Attached to
pathophysiology#Combined Neuronal and Muscle Dystonin Deficiency
The source establishes causal combined-isoform involvement, now represented by a qualified edge to contractures. It does not identify the relative neuronal and muscle mechanisms in the lethal cases; the separate DST-b-only cohort is not evidence of their biopsy or cardiac phenotype.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:40497796 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Genotype-phenotype correlation in these patients and previously published cases with respective variants resulted in the definition of a DST-associated lethal congenital contracture syndrome."
Establishes DST as the gene defining a lethal congenital contracture syndrome.
PMID:40497796 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Variants that also impact DST-a besides DST-b result in a more severe, lethal congenital contracture syndrome."
Gives the isoform-dependent severity relationship that separates the lethal phenotype from the congenital myopathy.
How much of the NEK9 skeletal phenotype results from primary developmental dysplasia versus reduced fetal movement?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN lccs10_developmental_attribution
Attached to
pathophysiology#Mitotic and Ciliary Regulatory Failure phenotypes#Short Long Bones
The original lethal families had akinesia and skeletal dysplasia, and their fibroblasts had both mitotic and ciliary abnormalities. Qualified causal links are represented. The unresolved issue is tissue and process attribution, not whether the causal genotype or the fetal movement phenotype exists. Surviving APUG-like cases are not used to supply LCCS10 visceral findings.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:26908619 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We report two Irish Traveller families with a previously undescribed lethal skeletal dysplasia characterized by fetal akinesia, shortening of all long bones, multiple contractures, rib anomalies, thoracic dysplasia, pulmonary hypoplasia and protruding abdomen."
The original lethal skeletal-dysplasia families establish the LCCS10 clinical scope; the later APUG-like neonates are not used as its denominator.
Why do human GLDN variants cause severe fetal disease when gliomedin-null mice have a milder nodal phenotype?
HUMAN MODEL MISMATCH OPEN lccs_gldn_model_fidelity
Attached to
pathophysiology#Node of Ranvier Assembly Failure
Human nerve abnormalities support the nodopathy mechanism, but preserved conduction and absence of arthrogryposis in null mice constrain a simple universal loss-of-function-to-complete-conduction-block model. Allele, species and compensatory nodal mechanisms remain unresolved.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Homozygous gldn−/− mice do not have apparent neurologic deficits or features of arthrogryposis and demonstrate normal nerve conduction and peripheral nervous system myelin (Feinberg, et al., 2010)."
The primary clinical paper summarizes the null-mouse contrast; this is model-organism evidence, not a negative finding in human GLDN disease.
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Nerve conduction velocity studies performed at 1 week of age demonstrated axonal motor and sensory neuropathy but were normal when repeated at 7 months of age."
Human longitudinal variability is another constraint on a permanent, uniform conduction-block model.
What causes hydrops and restricted growth in affected fetuses, and when do placental factors contribute?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN lccs_hydrops_growth
Attached to
phenotypes#Hydrops Fetalis phenotypes#Intrauterine Growth Restriction
The rat paralysis experiment supports a qualified akinesia-to-growth-restriction contribution, now represented in the graph. It does not resolve the relative contribution in the GLDN-associated fetus with abnormal umbilical-artery flow. The reviewed LCCS sources establish hydrops but do not identify a subtype-specific fluid-balance mechanism; its absence in original ERBB3 and PIP5K1C kindreds constrains an obligatory consequence of contractures.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35806855 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Sonography examination revealed hydrothorax, subcutaneous generalized edema, short long bones, fixed limb joints, absent fetal movements, fetal growth restriction (estimated fetal weight in the 4th percentile and absent end-diastolic flow in both umbilical arteries), and polyhydramnios (amniotic..."
The coexisting flow abnormality constrains attribution of growth restriction solely to the fetal motor disorder.
What causes jaw retrusion and hip dislocation in the reported LCCS patients?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN lccs_craniofacial_joint_distribution
Attached to
phenotypes#Retrognathia phenotypes#Hip Dislocation
Fetal immobility is a supported general deformation mechanism, with micrognathia and pterygia routes represented separately. The reviewed sources do not specifically resolve jaw retrusion or hip dislocation in the cited LCCS patients. A small mandible is not equivalent to retrusion, and local mechanical versus primary developmental attribution remains uncertain.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35806855 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The pregnancy was terminated and postmortem studies confirmed the prenatal findings: distal arthrogryposis, fetal growth restriction, pulmonary hypoplasia, and retrognathia."
The primary case supports jaw retrusion, not mandibular size.
Which central, peripheral autonomic or developmental lesion produces neurogenic bladder in ERBB3 and CNTNAP1 disease?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN lccs_bladder_localization
Attached to
phenotypes#Neurogenic Bladder
Human reports establish neurogenic bladder in both subtypes but do not localize the responsible autonomic lesion. Somatic anterior-horn loss alone does not establish the bladder mechanism; CNTNAP1 disease includes both central and peripheral pathology.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:29882456 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Individual LD_0333.0 was a girl with severe hypotonia and respiratory insufficiency that required a tracheostomy after birth, hypomyelinating neuropathy, bilateral hearing loss, bilateral optic nerve atrophy, lethargy, and neurogenic bladder."
Documents neurogenic bladder in a CNTNAP1-associated child, extending the subtype scope without identifying the responsible autonomic pathway.
Are GLDN-associated bulbar and diaphragmatic weakness and airway malacia caused by the same peripheral lesion?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN lccs_gldn_bulbar_airway
Attached to
phenotypes#Vocal Cord Paresis phenotypes#Diaphragmatic Paralysis phenotypes#Laryngomalacia phenotypes#Tracheomalacia phenotypes#Central Apnea
The source establishes focal paresis, diaphragm paralysis and laryngoscopic malacia in different children. Their downstream swallowing and respiratory contributions are represented where directly supported. It does not localize the upstream cause of paresis or establish that structural airway malacia is a direct consequence of gliomedin deficiency. Polysomnographic central apnea is separately reported; its coexistence with tracheomalacia does not establish an obstructive mechanism.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Insufficient swallowing aggravated by paresis of both the right vocal cord and right side of the soft palate prompted gastrostomy tube placement at 4 months of age."
Documents vocal cord paresis and its contribution to the swallowing failure.
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Laryngoscopy demonstrated laryngo- and tracheomalacia, floppy epiglottis, and pooling of oral secretions."
The laryngoscopic findings explicitly establish laryngomalacia in this child.
How do neuropathy, weakness, contractures and respiratory illness contribute separately to delayed motor acquisition in GLDN survivors?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN lccs_motor_trajectory
Attached to
phenotypes#Motor Developmental Delay
The source documents delayed milestones and subsequent gains with growth and care. It does not separate peripheral motor dysfunction from mechanical restriction, prolonged illness or developmental contributors sufficiently to assign a specific causal edge to motor delay.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Motor and language development was delayed; he was able to sit independently at age 13 months and started to crawl at age 24 months."
The exact ages establish motor delay in the Family 3 survivor, without inferring cognitive impairment.
What accounts for the distinct sensory and palatal findings in surviving CNTNAP1-associated children?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN lccs_cntnap1_sensory_palate
Attached to
phenotypes#Cerebral Visual Impairment phenotypes#Hearing Impairment phenotypes#Optic Atrophy phenotypes#Cleft Palate
Central and peripheral glial-axonal disease provides plausible context, but the report does not connect the observed cortical visual loss, hearing loss, optic atrophy or palatal malformation to a specific demonstrated mechanism. Hearing-loss localization and the developmental cause of cleft palate remain unresolved; the established central hypomyelination and atrophy routes are represented separately.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:29882456 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Individual LD_0158.0 is an 8 year old male with hypotonia and respiratory failure requiring intubation at birth and subsequent tracheotomy-dependency, congenital hypomyelinating neuropathy with arthrogryposis multiplex congenita, cleft palate, cortical vision loss, bilateral hearing loss, and..."
This patient-level clinical observation establishes the findings but does not establish a single causal route for them.
Were pulmonary hypertension and ductal patency in the DNM2 siblings primary developmental findings or consequences of respiratory insufficiency?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN lccs_dnm2_cardiopulmonary_attribution
Attached to
phenotypes#Pulmonary Hypertension phenotypes#Patent Ductus Arteriosus
The source explicitly states that primary versus secondary attribution could not be determined. The general developmental endocytosis hypothesis does not resolve this specific uncertainty, so no direct respiratory or genetic-mechanism edge is asserted.
Show evidence (1 reference)
url:https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC3658203/ SUPPORT Human Clinical
"AFB3 and AFB4 had pulmonary hypertension and a patent ductus arteriosus; whether these features were primary or secondary to respiratory insufficiency could not be determined."
The authors state the causal attribution limit for these two cardiopulmonary observations.
Did scoliosis in the GLDN survivor result from neuromuscular imbalance, fixed posture or a separate developmental process?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN lccs_scoliosis_attribution
Attached to
phenotypes#Scoliosis
The clinical course establishes scoliosis requiring surgery at age nine, alongside weakness, atrophy and congenital contractures. It does not identify the cause or provide serial spinal development sufficient to distinguish neuromuscular deformation from another developmental process. The surgery documents the phenotype, not a demonstrated causal intermediary.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"He underwent surgical treatment for bilateral hip luxation and clubfeet (age 2 years) and scoliosis with Harrington rod insertion (age 9 years)."
The GLDN Family 2 survivor underwent scoliosis surgery with Harrington rod insertion at age nine years. The hip procedure in the same sentence occurred at age two.
⚙

Pathophysiology

17
Nuclear mRNA Export Failure
GLE1 participates in nuclear mRNA export and translation. Human GLE1 variants cause LCCS1 with anterior-horn motor-neuron deficiency; zebrafish studies implicate dividing neural precursor survival and motor-axon arborization. The precise RNA targets and cell-autonomous contributions in affected human fetuses remain unresolved; a shared RNA pathway across LCCS1-3 remains a hypothesis.
GLE1 hgnc:4315 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves GLE1 (hgnc:4315). hgnc:4315 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
mRNA export from nucleus GO:0006406 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves decreased mRNA export from nucleus (GO:0006406). GO:0006406 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology. ↓ DECREASED
Downstream
Anterior Horn Motor Neuron Loss GLE1 dysfunction is linked to the human motor-neuron deficit, with neural precursor apoptosis supported in zebrafish. This is a qualified developmental route, not proof of death of differentiated human motor neurons.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:18204449 SUPPORT Other
"Our finding of mutated GLE1 exposes a common pathway connecting the genes implicated in LCCS1, LCCS2 and LCCS3 and elucidates mRNA processing as a critical molecular mechanism in motoneuron development and maturation."
Identifies mRNA processing as the molecular mechanism, and proposes it as the pathway shared across the anterior horn subtypes.
PI3K-Akt Signaling Loss
The LCCS2 ERBB3 splice variant predicts loss of receptor signaling. The authors propose reduced PI3K/Akt survival signaling as a cause of neuronal apoptosis; Erbb3-null mice also lack Schwann-cell precursors. These are mechanistic hypotheses supporting, but not completing, the human genotype-to-neurogenic disease route.
ERBB3 hgnc:3431 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves ERBB3 (hgnc:3431). hgnc:3431 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
phosphatidylinositol 3-kinase/protein kinase B signal transduction GO:0043491 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves decreased phosphatidylinositol 3-kinase/protein kinase B signal transduction (GO:0043491). GO:0043491 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology. ↓ DECREASED
Downstream
Anterior Horn Motor Neuron Loss Reduced ERBB3 survival signaling is a proposed cause of the documented LCCS2 anterior-horn lesion. The human splice finding and mouse survival phenotype support an indirect route; the contribution of glial support is unresolved.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:17701904 SUPPORT Other
"an activator of the phosphatidylinositol-3-kinase/Akt pathway--regulating cell survival and vesicle trafficking"
Names the pathway ERBB3 activates and the cellular functions it governs.
Phosphoinositide Synthesis Deficiency
The original PIP5K1C D253N allele markedly reduces recombinant PIPKI-gamma activity. The authors propose that reduced PIP2 impairs synaptic vesicle trafficking and neuronal function. Clinical similarity to LCCS2 does not demonstrate anterior-horn loss: the original LCCS3 families declined postmortem examination.
PIP5K1C hgnc:8996 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves PIP5K1C (hgnc:8996). hgnc:8996 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
1-phosphatidylinositol-4-phosphate 5-kinase activity GO:0016308 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this molecular function This pathophysiological event involves decreased 1-phosphatidylinositol-4-phosphate 5-kinase activity (GO:0016308). GO:0016308 is a molecular function from the Gene Ontology. ↓ DECREASED
Downstream
Absent Fetal Movement PIP2 deficiency is proposed to impair synaptic vesicle trafficking and neuronal function, reducing fetal motor activity. This qualified route does not assert unobserved anterior-horn pathology.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:17701898 SUPPORT Other
"Thus, a defect in the phosphatidylinositol pathway leading to a decrease in synthesis of PIP(2), a molecule active in endocytosis of synaptic vesicle proteins, culminates in lethal congenital arthrogryposis."
States the enzymatic deficiency, the downstream lipid, and its synaptic vesicle role in one sentence.
cAMP Synthesis Deficiency
ADCY6 encodes an adenylate cyclase. Human peripheral amyelination and zebrafish knockdown support a myelination role, likely through cAMP. Complete absence of cAMP production or the full signaling sequence was not measured in affected humans.
ADCY6 hgnc:237 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves ADCY6 (hgnc:237). hgnc:237 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
cAMP biosynthetic process GO:0006171 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves decreased cAMP biosynthetic process (GO:0006171). GO:0006171 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology. ↓ DECREASED
Downstream
Schwann Cell Myelination Failure ADCY6 dysfunction is proposed to impair the cAMP-dependent myelination program; the source qualifies the cAMP mediation as likely.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:24319099 SUPPORT Other
"ADCY6 encodes a protein that belongs to the adenylate cyclase family responsible for the synthesis of cAMP."
Identifies the enzymatic function lost in the ADCY6 subtype.
Mitotic and Ciliary Regulatory Failure
NEK9 p.Arg497* eliminates full-length protein in patient fibroblasts, with reduced proliferation, delayed cell-cycle progression, and fewer, shorter cilia. The authors propose a ciliopathy underlying the lethal skeletal dysplasia. The relative contributions of cell-cycle and ciliary dysfunction to fetal akinesia and skeletal growth are unresolved.
NEK9 hgnc:18591 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves NEK9 (hgnc:18591). hgnc:18591 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.

Absent Fetal Movement The NEK9-associated developmental disorder includes fetal akinesia; the ciliary or mitotic route to motor impairment remains provisional.

Anterior Horn Motor Neuron Loss
Anterior-horn motor-neuron deficiency or atrophy is documented in GLE1 and ERBB3 disease. Zebrafish Gle1 studies identify apoptosis of dividing neural precursors rather than differentiated motor neurons. This cellular route must not be generalized to PIP5K1C disease, whose original series lacked autopsies.

Neurogenic Skeletal Muscle Atrophy and Hypoplasia Loss of developing motor innervation is a plausible contributor to the severe muscle underdevelopment associated with the GLE1 autopsy lesion; the precise developmental sequence was not directly observed.
Skeletal Muscle Hypoplasia Severely deficient motor-neuron development is interpreted to deprive developing muscle of innervation in classic GLE1 disease. This is an indirect neurogenic interpretation of the paired human lesions.

Frames LCCS1 as an in utero motor neuron disease with spinal motoneuron atrophy and consequent immobility.

Records anterior horn atrophy as the neuropathological lesion in LCCS2 as well, extending the node beyond LCCS1.
PMID:22357925 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Unexpectedly, the motoneuron deficiency results from apoptosis of neural precursors, not of differentiated motoneurons."
Refines the cellular mechanism: the motoneuron loss is a precursor survival defect rather than degeneration of mature neurons.
Schwann Cell Myelination Failure
Peripheral myelination is deficient in ADCY6- and ADGRG6-associated disease. Human nerve pathology supports this lesion; ADCY6 knockdown and ADGRG6 studies in fish and mice inform the proposed signaling routes. Schwann precursor loss in Erbb3-null mice is a different model observation and is not asserted as measured human LCCS2 myelin pathology.

Demonstrates absent peripheral myelin in ADGRG6/GPR126-related disease in human tissue.
Node of Ranvier Assembly Failure
GLDN encodes gliomedin, which contributes to nodal sodium-channel clustering through axonal ligands including NF186. Human nodal abnormalities and variant assays support a nodopathy. GLDN-null mice can retain conduction and lack arthrogryposis, so complete conduction abolition cannot be inferred from loss of gliomedin alone.

PMID:27616481 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"These data indicate that mutations of GLDN or CNTNAP1 (MIM: 616286), encoding essential components of the nodes of Ranvier and paranodes, respectively, lead to inherited nodopathies, a distinct disease entity among peripheral neuropathies."
The authors place GLDN nodal and CNTNAP1 paranodal defects in the broader inherited-nodopathy category while explicitly distinguishing their anatomical sites. This node represents the GLDN nodal lesion; CNTNAP1 is modeled separately.
Impaired Motor Nerve Conduction
Peripheral motor signaling is impaired in CNTNAP1 and some GLDN cases. Original CNTNAP1 families had motor velocities below 10 m/s, but this threshold is not universal, and a GLDN child had normal repeat studies at seven months. The functional route to weakness and reduced movement is supported indirectly by the human neuropathy and nodal-pathology context.

Downstream
Absent Fetal Movement Impaired peripheral motor signaling contributes to prenatal akinesia in the documented nodopathies, without implying complete conduction failure or identical measurements in every subtype.
Muscle Weakness Peripheral motor neuropathy can reduce effective muscle activation in GLDN survivors; concurrent muscular contributions and changing severity remain possible.
Areflexia Impaired peripheral reflex-arc signaling provides an indirect explanation for absent reflexes in the GLDN neuropathy context; areflexia alone does not localize the lesion.
Skeletal Muscle Atrophy Chronic peripheral motor dysfunction can produce neurogenic loss of muscle bulk in GLDN survivors. Human findings also leave primary muscular contributions possible.

PMID:24319099 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Patients showed a marked reduction in motor nerve conduction velocity (<10 m/s)"
Quantifies the conduction failure in affected individuals.
Myofibril Assembly Failure
ZBTB42 knockdown causes myofibrillar disorganization and abnormal neuromuscular junctions in zebrafish, with impaired coiling and escape movement. Wild-type human ZBTB42, but not the variant construct, rescues the model phenotype. Human muscle biopsy and autopsy were declined, so the exact human tissue lesion remains inferred.

Endocytic Trafficking Failure
The DNM2 p.Phe379Val allele impairs endocytosis in assays and is associated with a severe recessive congenital syndrome. Zebrafish knockdown affects muscle organization and angiogenesis. The authors propose altered developmental endocytosis as an explanation for pleiotropic clinical manifestations, without establishing the specific human tissue route to each finding.

Downstream
Absent Fetal Movement Developmental trafficking dysfunction is proposed to impair motor function; zebrafish muscle disorganization supports, but does not prove, the human route.
Severe Hypotonia The authors propose a pleiotropic developmental mechanism for the DNM2 syndrome. The relative contributions of muscle and peripheral nerve dysfunction to hypotonia remain unresolved.
Intracranial Hemorrhage The developmental endocytosis hypothesis and zebrafish angiogenesis defects provide provisional support for a vascular contribution to brain hemorrhage; human vascular pathology and other bleeding contributors were not resolved.
Retinal Hemorrhage The proposed pleiotropic endocytosis mechanism may disrupt ocular vascular development. The retinal bleeding route is not directly reconstructed, and the platelet abnormalities in the siblings are an additional unresolved contributor.
Thin Ribs The authors propose altered developmental endocytosis as a pleiotropic explanation for the DNM2 skeletal findings. A primary skeletal effect versus secondary reduced mechanical loading is unresolved.

Supports the pleiotropic muscle-and-vascular role that separates this node from the purely myofibrillar one.
Neurogenic Skeletal Muscle Atrophy and Hypoplasia
Loss of effective developing motor innervation can impair muscle growth and contraction. GLE1 fetal autopsies show severely hypoplastic musculature together with anterior-horn deficiency. The causal interpretation is supported indirectly by fetal neurogenic pathology; PIP5K1C muscle wasting is not assigned to this specific lesion without autopsy evidence.

Impaired Swallowing
Impaired fetal deglutition reduces amniotic-fluid clearance. This prenatal mechanism is distinguished from postnatal swallowing insufficiency with documented focal bulbar paresis in a GLDN survivor.

PMID:35806855 SUPPORT Other
"The common feature of this sequence is decreased fetal activity, which leads to a failure of normal deglutition, resulting in polyhydramnios."
The source explicitly gives the fetal activity-to-deglutition-to-amniotic-fluid sequence.
Paranodal Axoglial Junction Failure
CNTNAP1/CASPR1 deficiency disrupts septate paranodal junctions in peripheral nerves and is proposed to disturb glial-axonal interactions centrally. This is anatomically distinct from gliomedin-dependent nodal assembly.
CNTNAP1 hgnc:8011 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves CNTNAP1 (hgnc:8011). hgnc:8011 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Downstream
Impaired Motor Nerve Conduction Loss of the paranodal junctional barrier disrupts axoglial organization required for rapid conduction; severe slowing was measured in affected CNTNAP1 children.
Central Nervous System Hypomyelination CNTNAP1-associated disruption of central glial-axonal interactions is implicated in the serially documented hypomyelination; the human central ultrastructure was not examined.
Brain Atrophy Central myelin abnormalities and possible neuronal loss are proposed contributors to progressive volume loss; their separate contributions are unresolved.
Seizures Paranodal disruption and consequent ion-channel mislocalization are proposed to promote seizures. Potassium-channel mislocalization is demonstrated in models, not in these human central nervous systems.
Severe Hypotonia The demonstrated congenital peripheral neuropathy provides a supported indirect contribution to severe hypotonia in CNTNAP1 disease; concurrent central involvement prevents exclusive peripheral attribution.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:29882456 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"These 2 families illustrate severe progressive peripheral demyelinating neuropathy due to the absence of septate paranodal junctions and central hypomyelination with neurodegeneration in CNTNAP1-associated arthrogryposis multiplex congenita."
The authors explicitly attribute progressive peripheral neuropathy to missing paranodal junctions and document associated central disease.
Loss of Slow Myosin-binding Protein C Function
Recessive MYBPC1 truncation abrogates functional protein domains. A muscle contractile route is supported by the protein role and human causal genotype, but the precise fetal muscle structural defect is not demonstrated.

PMID:22610851 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We now show that a recessive mutation abrogating all functional domains in the same gene leads to LCCS."
Establishes recessive MYBPC1 loss of function as the LCCS4 mechanism.
Combined Neuronal and Muscle Dystonin Deficiency
Biallelic DST variants affecting both DST-a and DST-b cause a lethal arthrogryposis presentation. The relative neuronal and muscle contributions are unresolved; functional and biopsy findings from DST-b-only myopathy cannot be assigned wholesale to this combined-isoform disorder.

Downstream
Multiple Congenital Joint Contractures Combined neuronal and muscle isoform disruption is causally associated with the severe congenital-contracture syndrome; the responsible tissue sequence remains unresolved.

Establishes DST as the gene defining a lethal congenital contracture syndrome.

Gives the isoform-dependent severity relationship that separates the lethal phenotype from the congenital myopathy.
Focal Bulbar Paresis
Right vocal-cord and right soft-palate paresis aggravated swallowing insufficiency in a GLDN survivor. The report establishes this functional contribution without identifying the upstream cranial motor lesion.

Medical Actions

7
Mechanical Ventilation and Chronic Respiratory Support
Action: Invasive Mechanical VentilationNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Invasive Mechanical Ventilation (NCIT:C191573). NCIT:C191573 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C191573
Platform: Device
Invasive ventilation and, in some survivors, tracheostomy with chronic respiratory support were reported in GLDN-associated disease. Some children later weaned from support. The observational series does not isolate the survival benefit from genotype, disease severity or other care. Mechanical ventilation substitutes for the respiratory effort that the hypoplastic lung and weak respiratory musculature cannot generate.
Target Phenotypes: Neonatal Respiratory Failure HP:0002878 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP) Relation: this treatment targets this phenotype This treatment targets Neonatal Respiratory Failure, annotated with Respiratory failure (HP:0002878). HP:0002878 is a phenotype from the Human Phenotype Ontology.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"four of whom survived beyond the neonatal period into infancy, childhood, and late adolescence with intensive care and chronic respiratory and nutritional support"
Reports the survival achieved with sustained respiratory support.
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Our findings confirm the association of GLDN mutations with AMC and extend the LCCS11 phenotype by describing long-term survival with chronic respiratory and nutritional support."
Reports long-term survival with respiratory and nutritional support; this is an observational care association, not proof that support alone determines survival.
Enteral Nutritional Support
Action: Enteral Tube FeedingNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Enteral Tube Feeding (NCIT:C93322). NCIT:C93322 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C93322
Platform: Device
Gastrostomy feeding supported a GLDN survivor with swallowing insufficiency aggravated by focal bulbar paresis. Oral feeding later increased, so indefinite tube dependence is not obligatory.
Mechanism Target:
BYPASSES Focal Bulbar Paresis — Tube feeding bypasses unsafe or insufficient oral intake caused partly by bulbar paresis; it does not reverse the paresis.

"Insufficient swallowing aggravated by paresis of both the right vocal cord and right side of the soft palate prompted gastrostomy tube placement at 4 months of age."
Documents insufficient swallowing and the gastrostomy it required in a surviving LCCS11 child.

Genetic Counseling and Prenatal Testing
Action: Genetic CounselingNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Genetic Counseling (NCIT:C15240). NCIT:C15240 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15240
Molecular diagnosis can inform recurrence counseling and reproductive testing in these recessive disorders. The usual recurrence estimate requires confirmed parental carrier status and the relevant family genotype.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:29959180 SUPPORT Other
"A genetic diagnosis has clinical utility as it may affect management and prognosis and informs recurrence risk, facilitating family planning decisions."
States the counselling and reproductive value of reaching a molecular diagnosis.
Contracture Management and Rehabilitation
Action: RehabilitationNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Rehabilitation (NCIT:C15315). NCIT:C15315 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15315
Platform: Behavioral / lifestyle
Physiotherapy and splinting improved contractures, range of motion and lower-limb tone in one GLDN survivor. Other survivors underwent selected orthopedic procedures. These observed courses do not establish a uniform protocol or irreversible contracture trajectory.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Physiotherapy and non-surgical treatment including splinting led to an improvement of his contractures, increased range of motion, and improved muscular tone in his lower limbs."
The Family 3 survivor improved with physiotherapy and splinting; this directly corrects the prior claim that contractures cannot improve.
Perinatal Palliative Care and Shared Goals of Care
Action: Perinatal palliative careNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Perinatal palliative care, annotated with Palliative Therapy (NCIT:C15292). NCIT:C15292 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Palliative Therapy NCIT:C15292
Multidisciplinary perinatal palliative care supported a family with prenatal ERBB3-associated LCCS2. The family initially requested supported comfort care, then accepted nasal oxygen, nasogastric feeding, thermoregulation and bladder catheterization when the infant continued to breathe. Shared goals can change with the observed course; palliative care does not imply comfort-only care or an identical prognosis across LCCS subtypes.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:40321344 SUPPORT Other
"Perinatal palliative care is principled to support families through this uncertainty using shared decision-making to establish goals of care."
This is the authors’ care principle for uncertain LCCS2 outcomes, not a measured treatment effect or a recommendation to withhold intensive care.
PMID:40321344 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"She had consultations with pediatric urology and a Perinatal Palliative Care Team including representatives from neonatology, pediatrics, Chaplaincy, among others. Given the family’s previous experience and the counseled expectations for infants born with LCCS2, patient requested a supported..."
The mother carrying the second affected sibling received multidisciplinary consultation and initially chose supported comfort care. This is an observed family decision, separate from the authors’ general principle.
PMID:40321344 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Despite expectations to allow a natural death after birth, she continued to have respiratory drive and live. The family’s goals of care pivoted to accept neonatal intensive level care with perceived non-aggressive interventions such as nasal cannula oxygen support, enteral feedings via..."
The second sibling’s continued respiratory drive changed the family’s goals. These actual noninvasive respiratory, nutritional and supportive interventions demonstrate that the care plan was revisable; they do not prove efficacy or a uniform survival trajectory.
Tracheostomy
Action: TracheostomyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Tracheostomy, annotated with Tracheotomy (NCIT:C15341). NCIT:C15341 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Tracheotomy NCIT:C15341
Tracheostomy provided access for respiratory support in selected GLDN-associated survivors with persistent respiratory insufficiency. The Family 3 child underwent the procedure at six weeks for respiratory insufficiency, ineffective cough and swallowing difficulty, then weaned from ventilation at nine months and was decannulated at 19 months. Other children remained dependent; the procedure does not itself reverse pulmonary hypoplasia or neuromuscular weakness.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Due to persistent respiratory insufficiency, inability to cough, and insufficient swallowing, he underwent tracheostomy at 6 weeks of age. His respiratory function gradually improved, and he was successfully weaned from ventilatory support at nine months of age and subsequently decannulated at..."
This is the Family 3 survivor’s indication and subsequent course, not evidence that tracheostomy caused recovery or that decannulation is expected in every child.
Selected Orthopedic Surgery
Action: Selected orthopedic surgeryNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Selected orthopedic surgery, annotated with Orthopedic Surgical Procedure (NCIT:C16186). NCIT:C16186 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Orthopedic Surgical Procedure NCIT:C16186
A GLDN-associated Family 2 survivor underwent operations for bilateral hip luxation and clubfeet at two years and scoliosis instrumentation at nine years. These procedures addressed selected structural problems alongside respiratory, nutritional and rehabilitation care; the case does not isolate a surgical benefit or establish a standard operative plan for LCCS.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"He underwent surgical treatment for bilateral hip luxation and clubfeet (age 2 years) and scoliosis with Harrington rod insertion (age 9 years)."
The Family 2 survivor had these specific operations at distinct ages. Later mobility and respiratory changes occurred during a broader course of care and are not attributed solely to surgery.
🔬

Diagnosis

9
Prenatal ultrasound (PRESENT)
Ultrasound can detect impaired movement, fixed joints, abnormal fluid volume, growth restriction and associated anomalies. Classic Finnish LCCS was recognizable from 12 weeks in the cited series, but GLDN cases were often detected much later; no universal timing or specificity is established.
Prenatal diagnostic ultrasound NCIT:C19337 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35806855 SUPPORT Other
"Prenatal ultrasound imaging is crucial in its early diagnosis by identifying fetal movement limitations and the presence of club foot or joint contractures"
States the role and yield of prenatal ultrasound.
Trio exome or genome sequencing (PRESENT)
Broad molecular testing is useful for genetically heterogeneous fetal akinesia. The cited sequencing cohort solved 18 of 38 families with mixed fetal akinesia, arthrogryposis and congenital myopathy; this is not a LCCS-specific diagnostic sensitivity, and the cited evidence does not compare trio genome with exome sequencing.
Exome or genome sequencing NCIT:C15709 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:26578207 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"A conclusive genetic diagnosis was achieved for 18 of the 38 families."
The 18/38 yield is for a mixed neuromuscular cohort, not a denominator consisting only of LCCS.
Nerve conduction study (PRESENT)
Electrophysiology can characterize peripheral neuropathy in surviving infants. Velocities below 10 m/s were reported in the original CNTNAP1 families; they do not define all axoglial LCCS, and GLDN studies may normalize over time.
Motor nerve conduction studies NCIT:C88502 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:24319099 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Patients showed a marked reduction in motor nerve conduction velocity (<10 m/s)"
The threshold is an observation in the original CNTNAP1 families, not a validated diagnostic discriminator across all LCCS subtypes.
Fetal and neonatal autopsy (PRESENT)
Postmortem examination can document lung, muscle, spinal cord and peripheral nerve pathology and confirm prenatal findings. Acceptance and tissue availability vary; absent autopsy data must not be replaced by neuropathology inferred from clinical similarity.
Fetal or neonatal autopsy NCIT:C25153 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35806855 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"postmortem studies confirmed the prenatal findings: distal arthrogryposis, fetal growth restriction, pulmonary hypoplasia, and retrognathia"
Shows postmortem confirmation of the prenatal findings.
Fetal and postnatal magnetic resonance imaging (PRESENT)
MRI can characterize central nervous system involvement, but findings depend on subtype and age. A GLDN-affected fetus had normal brain and spinal cord MRI at 32 weeks, whereas serial brain MRI in two surviving CNTNAP1-associated children documented hypomyelination and progressive atrophy. A normal fetal scan does not exclude LCCS, and these reports do not establish a universal imaging schedule.
Fetal and postnatal MRI NCIT:C16809 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Fetal MRI performed at 32 weeks showed normal brain and spinal cord images."
This was the affected female infant in Family 4, whose prenatal ultrasound showed arthrogryposis and pulmonary hypoplasia; the normal central scan did not exclude GLDN-associated disease.
PMID:29882456 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Serial brain magnetic resonance images revealed hypomyelination, progressive atrophy, and reduced diffusion in the globus pallidus in both patients."
The two CNTNAP1-associated children had serial imaging abnormalities. This supports characterization of central involvement in that subtype, not a mandatory MRI pattern across all LCCS.
Electromyography (PRESENT)
EMG can help characterize a neurogenic component when interpreted with nerve conduction studies. Reduced motor unit recruitment accompanied slowed conduction in CNTNAP1-associated child LD_0158.0 at three weeks, whereas EMG was normal in the GLDN Family 3 survivor. These observations do not establish sensitivity or make a normal EMG exclusionary.
Electromyography NCIT:C38056 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:29882456 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Nerve conduction studies and electromyography (EMG) at 3 weeks of age showed prolongation of distal latencies and slow nerve conduction velocity (11.1 m/s at distal median) with decreased motor unit recruitment suggestive of a neurogenic disorder with features consistent with a demyelinating..."
The combined electrophysiological assessment in LD_0158.0 distinguished a neurogenic peripheral process; conduction velocity belongs to NCS, whereas reduced recruitment is the EMG contribution.
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Electromyography did not show any pathological finding."
EMG was normal in the GLDN Family 3 survivor despite clinical disease; this is a patient-specific limitation, not evidence against the diagnostic procedure.
Muscle biopsy (PRESENT)
Muscle histology may characterize the neuromuscular phenotype but can be normal or nonspecific. The GLDN Family 3 survivor had a normal muscle biopsy. In CNTNAP1-associated child LD_0158, quadriceps biopsy changed from mild scattered atrophy at one month to severe type I fiber atrophy and type II predominance at 3.5 years, mimicking congenital myopathy. A muscle pattern alone does not establish a primary myopathic LCCS mechanism.
Muscle Biopsy NCIT:C51895 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Muscle biopsy and MRI of the brain and spinal cord were normal."
The muscle biopsy was normal in the GLDN Family 3 survivor; the separately normal MRI is not a histological result.
PMID:29882456 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"A right quadriceps biopsy at 1 month of age showed mild atrophy of scattered muscle fibers without evidence of inflammation, myonecrosis, regeneration, or structural abnormalities (Figure 2). The fiber type distribution appeared normal (Figure 2A). A second muscle biopsy from the left quadriceps..."
Serial biopsies in LD_0158 show age-dependent histological change in CNTNAP1-associated neuropathy. The later myopathy-like pattern must be interpreted alongside the peripheral nerve findings.
Peripheral nerve biopsy (PRESENT)
Sural nerve histology and ultrastructure characterized progressive peripheral neuropathy in CNTNAP1-associated child LD_0158. The initial biopsy at one month showed mildly reduced myelinated axons, while repeat biopsy at 3.5 years showed severe demyelination and predominant axonal loss. This documents a selected patient investigation, not a routine biopsy recommendation or a finding established in every subtype.
Peripheral nerve biopsy NCIT:C217131 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:29882456 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"His initial sural nerve biopsy (right sural) at 1 month of age showed a mildly reduced number of myelinated axons (Figure 3A)."
This initial peripheral nerve biopsy belongs to LD_0158; it is distinct from his muscle biopsy and from the normal muscle biopsy in a GLDN survivor.
PMID:29882456 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"A second sural nerve biopsy (left sural) at 3.5 years of age showed severe demyelination and axonal pathology. The predominant pathology was axonal loss (Figure 3B)."
The repeat nerve biopsy documents progression in the same CNTNAP1-associated child, without supplying an umbrella diagnostic threshold.
Renal and bladder ultrasound (PRESENT)
Ultrasound documented urinary tract involvement in two ERBB3-associated siblings. The first had a distended bladder, bilateral distal ureteral dilatation and left renal calyceal dilation; the second had normal kidneys with a distended, mildly thick-walled bladder and required catheter drainage. Their megacystis was interpreted as neurogenic rather than mechanical obstruction; a keyhole appearance alone was not sufficient to establish obstruction.
Renal and bladder ultrasound NCIT:C159885 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:40321344 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Renal ultrasound revealed an overly distended bladder, bilateral distal ureteral dilatation, and left renal calyceal dilation consistent with urinary tract dilation classification P3."
This was the first affected sibling. Her ureteral and calyceal dilatation must not be assigned to the second sibling, whose kidneys appeared normal.
PMID:40321344 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Renal ultrasound showed normal kidneys with a distended bladder with mild wall thickening. She required an indwelling Foley catheter for adequate bladder emptying."
The second affected sibling had normal-appearing kidneys despite bladder dysfunction requiring drainage. The paper interprets LCCS2 megacystis as neurogenic, despite its obstructive-looking prenatal appearance.
PMID:40321344 SUPPORT Other
"In LCCS2, megacystis is not due to obstruction, despite its sonographic appearance, but rather to neurogenic causes."
The authors interpret the LCCS2 bladder phenotype as neurogenic despite an obstructive-looking ultrasound appearance. This interpretive statement distinguishes the sonographic finding from a demonstrated mechanical obstruction; it does not identify the responsible neural pathway.
📊

Prevalence

3
Finland
Birth Prevalence 3.96 per 100,000 1–9 per 100,000 (births) LCCS1
Finnish national ascertainment of all multiple-contracture cases 1987-2002, including live births, stillbirths and terminations. The source states the rate two ways, as 1 in 25,250 and as 0.40 per 10,000; both normalize to about 3.96 per 100,000, which is the figure recorded here.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:16892327 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"LCCS is noted as a unique Finnish disorder with a prevalence of 1 in 25,250 (0.40/10,000) births and is a major cause of lethal arthrogryposis in Finland."
Gives the Finnish birth prevalence of LCCS (the Finnish-heritage LCCS1 form) directly.
Finland
Carrier Frequency 1000.0 per 100,000 >1 in 1,000 (carriers) LCCS1
Carrier frequency of the GLE1 FinMajor founder allele in 200 healthy Finnish controls.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:18204449 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"we found that the carrier frequency of the LCCS1 FinMajormutation in the general Finnish population was 1%"
Gives the FinMajor carrier frequency in the general Finnish population.
Northeastern Finland
Carrier Frequency 2000.0 per 100,000 >1 in 1,000 (carriers) LCCS1
Carrier frequency in the region from which most LCCS1 families originate - double the national figure, and the reason regional ancestry matters to counselling.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:18204449 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The carrier frequency in northeastern Finland, where most of the families with LCCS1 originate, was 2%."
Gives the regionally enriched carrier frequency in the LCCS1 founder population.
⚖️

Clinical Burden

High
Severe fetal contractures and respiratory impairment cause high prenatal and neonatal mortality in many reported families. Surviving GLDN- and CNTNAP1-associated individuals may require prolonged respiratory, nutritional and mobility support. Outcome varies by genotype, family and clinical course, and the historical lethal label is not an invariant prognosis.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We report six infants and children from four unrelated families with biallelic GLDN mutations, four of whom survived beyond the neonatal period into infancy, childhood, and late adolescence with intensive care and chronic respiratory and nutritional support."
Documents substantial respiratory and nutritional care needs among the surviving GLDN children; the series does not establish that this subtype is uniformly the least severe.
🔀

Differential Diagnoses

4

Conditions with similar clinical presentations that must be differentiated from Lethal Congenital Contracture Syndrome:

Lethal Arthrogryposis with Anterior Horn Cell Disease
Overlapping Features GLE1-associated AAHD shares fetal akinesia, contractures and motor-neuron disease with classic LCCS1 but includes survival into childhood. The proposed coiled-coil-domain relationship is a hypothesis, not a definitive prognostic test for an individual allele.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:28884921 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Both LCCS and LAAHD are characterized by reduced fetal movements, congenital contractures, and a severe form of motor neuron disease that results in fetal death or death in the perinatal period, respectively."
States the shared phenotype and the timing difference that is the whole basis of the distinction.
PMID:28884921 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The phenotype of LAAHD is now expanded to include multiple individuals surviving into childhood suggesting that LAAHD is a misnomer and should be re-named Arthrogryposis with Anterior Horn Cell Disease (AAHD)."
Documents the survival that undermines the "lethal" label on the allelic disorder as well as on this one.
Arthrogryposis Multiplex Congenita
Overlapping Features AMC is the broader descriptive phenotype of congenital contractures in multiple body regions and has diverse genetic and non-genetic causes. LCCS is a historically numbered recessive subset; lethality alone does not delimit it.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:28726266 SUPPORT Other
"AMC results from diverse mechanisms, including any condition that decreases or limits fetal movements."
States the breadth of AMC against which LCCS must be positioned.
KIF21A-related fetal akinesia
Overlapping Features Biallelic KIF21A loss-of-function variants cause severe fetal akinesia with arthrogryposis. This is a molecular differential for the historically numbered LCCS syndromes, distinct from dominant KIF21A-associated congenital fibrosis of extraocular muscles.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34740919 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Together, these molecular and phenotypical data are in line with bi-allelic KIF21A PTVs being implicated in the pathogenesis of a severe form of autosomal recessive FA characterised by arthrogryposis multiplex, pulmonary hypoplasia and variable facial dysmorphisms."
The human genotype and fetal phenotype data support this molecular differential, rather than only a generic fetal-akinesia gene count.
Non-genetic fetal akinesia
Overlapping Features Extrinsic constraint, maternal disease or exposures can reduce fetal movement. The cited ungenotyped autopsy series illustrates etiological heterogeneity and incomplete ascertainment; a negative genetic test does not itself prove a non-genetic cause.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:11810649 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The etiology of akinesia was undetermined in Case 3; no extrinsic or intrinsic cause was identified."
An unresolved cause in an ungenotyped fetus demonstrates diagnostic uncertainty, not positive evidence for a particular extrinsic cause.

click to show 
name: Lethal Congenital Contracture Syndrome
creation_date: "2026-08-28T00:00:00Z"
category: Mendelian
description: >-
  Lethal congenital contracture syndrome (LCCS) comprises genetically heterogeneous autosomal recessive disorders
  with prenatal akinesia and congenital contractures. The numbered subtypes involve GLE1-associated spinal motor-neuron
  development, ERBB3 signaling, PIP5K1C-dependent phosphoinositide metabolism, MYBPC1-associated muscle function,
  DNM2-dependent trafficking, ZBTB42-associated muscle development, CNTNAP1 paranodal junctions, ADCY6 and ADGRG6
  peripheral myelination, NEK9-associated skeletal development, GLDN nodal organization, and combined neuronal and
  muscle DST isoforms. Some mechanistic steps are inferred from cellular or animal models and should not be transferred
  between subtypes. Reduced movement can produce joint deformations, impaired fetal swallowing and pulmonary hypoplasia,
  but primary skeletal abnormalities and central nervous system involvement also occur. Prenatal and neonatal mortality
  is high in selected series; GLDN- and CNTNAP1-associated disease can permit longer survival with substantial morbidity.
synonyms:
- LCCS
- Multiple contracture syndrome, Finnish type
- Herva disease

    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "A homozygous missense mutation in adenylate cyclase 6 gene (ADCY6) was found in another family characterized
      by a lack of myelin in the peripheral nervous system (PNS) as determined by TEM."
    explanation: Establishes ADCY6 and the amyelinating peripheral nerve phenotype.
- name: LCCS9

      homozygous mutation (one missense, one nonsense, and one frameshift mutation) in GPR126."
    explanation: Establishes biallelic GPR126/ADGRG6 variants as a cause of lethal AMC.

- name: LCCS10
  display_name: LCCS10 (NEK9-related)
  subtype_term:
    preferred_term: NEK9-related lethal skeletal dysplasia
    term:
      id: MONDO:0014870
      label: NEK9-related lethal skeletal dysplasia
  genes:
  - preferred_term: NEK9
    term:
      id: hgnc:18591
      label: NEK9
  description: >-
    NEK9-associated lethal skeletal dysplasia was initially reported in two Irish Traveller families with homozygous
    p.Arg497*, fetal akinesia, shortened long bones, rib and thoracic abnormalities, contractures and pulmonary
    hypoplasia. Patient fibroblasts show cell-cycle and ciliary abnormalities. These support a developmental mechanism
    without establishing how much each skeletal finding reflects primary dysplasia versus impaired movement. Later
    surviving APUG-like neonatal cases are a distinct allelic presentation.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:26908619
    reference_title: Recessive NEK9 mutation causes a lethal skeletal dysplasia with evidence of cell cycle
      and ciliary defects.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: We report two Irish Traveller families with a previously undescribed lethal skeletal dysplasia
      characterized by fetal akinesia, shortening of all long bones, multiple contractures, rib anomalies,
      thoracic dysplasia, pulmonary hypoplasia and protruding abdomen.
    explanation: The original lethal skeletal-dysplasia families establish the LCCS10 clinical scope; the
      later APUG-like neonates are not used as its denominator.
- name: LCCS11

  description: >-
    Caused by biallelic GLDN variants. Gliomedin is the Schwann-cell-derived

      by lethal AMC, we identified biallelic mutations in GLDN in the affected individuals."
    explanation: Establishes biallelic GLDN variants as the LCCS11 cause.

clinical_burden:
  burden_level: HIGH
  rationale: >-
    Severe fetal contractures and respiratory impairment cause high prenatal and neonatal mortality in many reported
    families. Surviving GLDN- and CNTNAP1-associated individuals may require prolonged respiratory, nutritional
    and mobility support. Outcome varies by genotype, family and clinical course, and the historical lethal label
    is not an invariant prognosis.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:28726266
    reference_title: "Survival among children with \"Lethal\" congenital contracture syndrome 11 caused by novel
      mutations in the gliomedin gene (GLDN)."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "We report six infants and children from four unrelated families with biallelic GLDN mutations, four
      of whom survived beyond the neonatal period into infancy, childhood, and late adolescence with intensive care
      and chronic respiratory and nutritional support."
    explanation: >-
      Documents substantial respiratory and nutritional care needs among the surviving GLDN children; the series
      does not establish that this subtype is uniformly the least severe.

pathophysiology:
- name: Nuclear mRNA Export Failure
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  subtypes:
  - LCCS1
  description: >-
    GLE1 participates in nuclear mRNA export and translation. Human GLE1 variants cause LCCS1 with anterior-horn
    motor-neuron deficiency; zebrafish studies implicate dividing neural precursor survival and motor-axon arborization.
    The precise RNA targets and cell-autonomous contributions in affected human fetuses remain unresolved; a shared
    RNA pathway across LCCS1-3 remains a hypothesis.
  biological_processes:
  - preferred_term: mRNA export from nucleus
    term:
      id: GO:0006406
      label: mRNA export from nucleus
    modifier: DECREASED
  genes:
  - preferred_term: GLE1
    term:
      id: hgnc:4315
      label: GLE1
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:18204449
    reference_title: Mutations in mRNA export mediator GLE1 result in a fetal motoneuron disease.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: "Our finding of mutated GLE1 exposes a common pathway connecting the genes implicated in LCCS1, LCCS2
      and LCCS3 and elucidates mRNA processing as a critical molecular mechanism in motoneuron development and maturation."
    explanation: >-
      Identifies mRNA processing as the molecular mechanism, and proposes it as
      the pathway shared across the anterior horn subtypes.
  downstream:
  - target: Anterior Horn Motor Neuron Loss
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      GLE1 dysfunction is linked to the human motor-neuron deficit, with neural precursor apoptosis supported in
      zebrafish. This is a qualified developmental route, not proof of death of differentiated human motor neurons.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:22357925
      reference_title: A zebrafish model of lethal congenital contracture syndrome 1 reveals Gle1 function in
        spinal neural precursor survival and motor axon arborization.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: Unexpectedly, the motoneuron deficiency results from apoptosis of neural precursors, not of
        differentiated motoneurons.
      explanation: The zebrafish perturbation identifies a precursor-survival route; translation to the human
        histological deficit is indirect.
      directness: INDIRECT
- name: PI3K-Akt Signaling Loss
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  subtypes:
  - LCCS2
  description: >-
    The LCCS2 ERBB3 splice variant predicts loss of receptor signaling. The authors propose reduced PI3K/Akt survival
    signaling as a cause of neuronal apoptosis; Erbb3-null mice also lack Schwann-cell precursors. These are mechanistic
    hypotheses supporting, but not completing, the human genotype-to-neurogenic disease route.

    explanation: >-
      Establishes DST as the gene defining a lethal congenital contracture
      syndrome.

      myopathy."
    supports: SUPPORT

    explanation: >-

phenotypes:
- category: Prenatal
  name: Absent Fetal Movement
  description: >-
    Reduced or absent prenatal movement across the LCCS spectrum. Total immobility at every
    ultrasound examination from 12 weeks or later characterized all 39 clinically defined Finnish
    LCCS cases; the frequency band refers to that series, not all genotypes or all gestational
    examinations. Reduced movement is a shared functional consequence of distinct motor-unit
    disorders and can also accompany NEK9 skeletal dysplasia. It can cause a fetal deformation
    sequence without explaining every primary skeletal, cerebral, vascular or visceral
    manifestation.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Absent fetal movement
    term:
      id: HP:0001558
      label: Decreased fetal movement
  frequency: VERY_FREQUENT
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:16892327
    reference_title: Lethal congenital contracture syndrome (LCCS) and other lethal arthrogryposes in
      Finland--an epidemiological study.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "Thirty-nine cases had lethal congenital contracture syndrome (LCCS) clinically characterized by total
      immobility of the fetus at all ultrasound examinations (12 weeks or later)"
    explanation: >-
      Establishes total fetal immobility from 12 weeks as a defining clinical
      feature of LCCS in a population-based series.
  notes: >-
    This clinical state was previously annotated at organism scale in a duplicate pathophysiology
    entry.

      snippet: 'Rat fetuses were paralyzed by daily transuterine injections of curare from day 18 of gestation until

        pulmonary hypoplasia, micrognathia, fetal growth retardation, short umbilical cords, and polyhydramnios.
        Neither sham-operated nor untouched littermate control fetuses had any of these anomalies.'
      explanation: Curare-exposed rat fetuses developed these abnormalities while sham-operated and untouched
        littermates did not. This supports an immobilization mechanism, with species and drug-exposure limits;
        it does not establish a numbered LCCS genotype.

- category: Respiratory
  name: Pulmonary Hypoplasia
  description: >-
    Underdeveloped lungs documented in several subtypes. A GLDN literature review found pulmonary
    hypoplasia in 12 of 16 cases with reported respiratory findings among 28 total cases; this
    selected reporting denominator cannot estimate prevalence across all GLDN cases or the LCCS
    umbrella. Deficient fetal respiratory-muscle movement can impair lung development; thoracic
    dysplasia is an additional potential contributor in NEK9 disease. Lung underdevelopment can
    cause respiratory insufficiency but does not account for all respiratory impairment in
    survivors.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Pulmonary hypoplasia
    term:
      id: HP:0002089
      label: Pulmonary hypoplasia
    located_in:
      preferred_term: lung
      term:
        id: UBERON:0002048
        label: lung
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:35806855
    reference_title: "Lethal Congenital Contracture Syndrome 11: A Case Report and Literature Review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "To our knowledge, among the herein 28 reviewed cases, 16 reported respiratory findings, pulmonary
      hypoplasia being the most frequent (75%, 12/16), followed by pulmonary insufficiency or need of respiratory
      support."
    explanation: >-
      The 75% proportion is 12/16 respiratory-described cases, not 12/28 or an umbrella prevalence estimate.
  notes: >-
    This clinical state was previously annotated at organism scale in a duplicate pathophysiology
    entry. The reduced-development annotation is lung development (GO:0030324), modifier DECREASED;
    this process annotation is retained here because phenotypes do not have a biological_processes
    slot.
  sequelae:
  - target: Neonatal Respiratory Failure
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_KNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: A GLDN survivor had persistent respiratory insufficiency associated with hypoplastic lungs
      and diaphragm paralysis. Both factors may contribute, and their relative effects were not quantified.

        novel mutations in the gliomedin gene (GLDN).
      supports: SUPPORT

      explanation: Documents diaphragm paralysis and its respiratory context in the Family 2 child.
- category: Respiratory
  name: Neonatal Respiratory Failure
  description: >-
    Respiratory failure at or soon after birth is documented in multiple LCCS subtypes. The original
    PIP5K1C kindred had five liveborn affected infants, all dying of respiratory insufficiency
    within minutes to hours; this is not an umbrella mortality estimate. GLDN cases demonstrate
    variable contributions from pulmonary hypoplasia, diaphragm paralysis and airway abnormalities,
    with central apnea separately documented in one child. Absent respiratory drive is not
    established across all subtypes, and some survivors later wean from ventilation.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Respiratory failure
    term:
      id: HP:0002878
      label: Respiratory failure

    explanation: Documents neonatal death from respiratory failure in an LCCS3 case.

    reference_title: Lethal contractural syndrome type 3 (LCCS3) is caused by a mutation in PIP5K1C, which
      encodes PIPKI gamma of the phophatidylinsitol pathway.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL

    explanation: Five of the nine affected pregnancies in the original PIP5K1C kindred were live births;

- category: Neurologic
  name: Reduced Motor Nerve Conduction Velocity
  subtype: LCCS7
  description: >-
